# M5 plan: the first dungeon

The plan of record for [m5-first-dungeon.md](m5-first-dungeon.md) (the spec: goal, DoD, research). Section numbers
are stable: append, never renumber, so briefs can cite "§4.2". The process (§6) is M4's (m4-plan.md §6, M3's P1-P8
with the model mix of D18 below): one fresh lead session per item at high effort, slices under 300K of context in
their own worktrees, merges and doc passes on the cheapest model, one review per item, the live gate by the lead alone,
frames judged solo (M3 D42), a commit per slice through `tools/commit.ps1`. The rules are CLAUDE.md, "Hard rules" and
"Rules for agent sessions"; a brief cites them and restates nothing. M4's decisions are cited as "M4 D<n>"
(m4-plan.md §7); this plan's own are D1-D24 (§7).

## 0. Status

Planned 2026-10-25 on master bfe466b7 (M4 complete; the hero video merged). The owner's answers are D1-D11 and D25-D28;
the planner's defaults D12-D24 may be overruled at any checkpoint. Budget (D12): the plan is sized in shares of the
owner's weekly meter, 40% build plus about 2% planning, against a 45% stop line; the meter resets on 2026-10-26.
**Item 0 is done** (2026-10-25, merged as "Merge m5/0 (The measuring run and the two live bugs)"; §8): the den's numbers
measured, the client-side cull, the runner's one-waypoint route, Godot's errors judged 0, the soak line. What it changed
for the items after it: the arrival floor is respawn-bound (D25: the whole den and the timed return, item 2), a level-80
mage casts VI (D26), and the fight's mana order is the stamina-to-mana dance (D27, item 1).
**Item 1 is done** (2026-10-26, merged as "Merge m5/1 (The outcome-aware caster and mana inside the fight)"; §8):
one cast policy for every automated caster, each automated cast sent as `Automation` (the kill switch stops a buff
pass), the Mana Conversion model, the try floor and the stamina-to-mana dance in one mana order for the keeper, the buff
cycle and the fight; the fight caps its level (`--fight-max-level`), runs unlimited kills to a clean `--fight-minutes`
end and reports its rates. The live read: no mana wait in 30 minutes from 9 mana, the arrival floor still respawn-bound
(68 kills an hour, 240 while targets stand). The gate's journeys run uncapped (D28). **Wave 1's checkpoint is answered (D37,
2026-10-26): go as planned.** Next: item 2 (§3, wave 2's first item, which slices wave 2), whose lead
prompt is `cache/m5-w2-lead-prompt.md`.
**Item 6 is done** (2026-10-26, run beside item 1 under D30 and merged second as "Merge m5/6 (Retail for the person at
the keys, and the two sizes)"; §8): the server's Yes/No questions shown and answered, the 49 character options saved on
the server, the window layout and the chat masks kept per character and window size, the spell bars editable with
retail's magic, melee and missile keys, retail's slash commands with tells and the two recalls, the public property
updates (a chest's lock read honestly), every login message decoded, the camera following a resize, and the shell frames
and play-checks at 1280x720 and 1920x1080 (report-only until item 7's gate, D36). Items 2-5 and 7-8 remain; the wave
order of §3 stands (item 6 was first on the cut list, D17, and is now bought).
**Item 2 is done** (2026-10-27, merged as "Merge m5/2 (The hunting-ground profile and the whole den)"; §8): every
hunting ground is a JSON profile read by one loader (the den, the belt, the forest, and Tusker Temple as a second dungeon
written as data only, D46), drafted from the dats and the world SQL by one command; the room graph over the den's six
storeys with the wing no-go; the indoor runner (it runs through a dungeon's doorways) and the creature layer indoors; the
respawn schedule, the tour down and back with the timed return, and a walk that always has a purpose or holds (D47). The
30-minute read: 224 kills an hour over five storeys (item 0's arrival floor alone: 68). The owner's D45 (a solid world
mandatory, item 5), D46 and D47 came during the item. Wave 2 is sliced as §4.4 and §4.5 (D39-D44, the owner's D38 in
item 3's rows). Next: item 3 (§4.5, wave 2's last item, which writes checkpoint 2), whose lead prompt is
`cache/m5-w3-lead-prompt.md`.
**Item 3 is done** (2026-10-28, merged as "Merge m5/3 (The mage's logistics and the death handler, cheat-free)"; §8):
every profile carries a `supplies` section (the stock lines, the vendor kit, the burden line, the purse as coin and
trade notes, D48, D49) read by the build, the counters and the stations; the mage's kit is a vendor robe and wand with
the four foci; the loot step is built whole and off (D38); the resupply station buys from the vendor whose `sells` lists
the ware; the experience is spent by the planner's order; every mod verb after the build is counted at one seam and the
journey grants and places nothing (the tie moved into the build, D50); the death is one handler (D51) that rearms (D53),
recalls to the corpse and takes everything back, with no vitae grant and no decoys. The live gate needed four rounds
(the fourth on the owner's word): round 4's mage journey 12 of 12 with `granted=0 places=0`, the forced trip `bought=27
spent_xp=164985199`, the death recovered by a bought wand. Wave 2 is complete. **Checkpoint 2 is answered (D54,
2026-10-28): go**, with items 4 and 5 at the same time, each under its own lead (`cache/m5-w4-lead-prompt.md`,
`cache/m5-w5-lead-prompt.md`), and the soak an overnight run.
**Item 4 is done** (2026-10-28, beside item 5 under D54, merged as "Merge m5/4 (The outer loop and the dial)"; §8): the
autopilot is one state machine over the journey's own stations (D57) with the open-ended fight, the upkeep, the trip, the
death scripted or not, the checkpoint, the watchdogs and recoveries, the reconnect and the stop rules; the dial is Num Lock
(D59) and the tools panel's button, the person's keys interrupt it and hand it back, the kill switch withdraws every
automated send; the gate runs a 30-minute autopilot lane and judges the mage's `granted=0 places=0` and DoD 6's and 7's
live fields from this gate (D55, D56). Round 2's lane: 89 kills in 30 minutes, one death recovered, one trip, one hand-off,
`ok=yes`.

**Item 5 is done** (2026-10-28, merged first of wave 3 as "Merge m5/5 (A solid world)"; §8): every server object with collision is a physics object in the player's world (D70, D71), creatures run retail's transitions through it and collide with each other, the player, doors and lifestones (D45, D72), a closed door on a route is opened and walked through (`door_opened=yes` at Renald's shop live), a recall lands beside the lifestone (`stepped_off=no` live, D73), the planner judges the fixed world (F3) and the interpolation runs at retail's rate (D74). The den's tuskers no longer stand on one spot; the den fight's blips are a named finding for the owner (`m5-den-creature-blips`: 62 in 108 s, retail's rule with ACE's latency). Item 4 merged second and wrote checkpoint 3 (D54).
**Checkpoint 3 is written** (2026-10-28, §8): wave 3 took about 7% against its 11%, and the milestone lands near 43%
against the 45% stop line. **Checkpoint 3 is answered (D85, 2026-10-28): go**, item 7 now inside one 12-hour window (the
soak a run of about five hours, not a night); its lead prompt `cache/m5-w7-lead-prompt.md`.
**Item 7 is done** (2026-10-31, merged as "Merge m5/7 (The overnight run)"; §8): the long run (606 minutes, 1,910 kills,
0 deaths), the owner's hand-off by /stop and /auto, every open issue resolved before the merge (D148), the item-end
gates on fresh characters, the login pause (D151) and the walker's lost keys (D153). Item 8, "The end", is next; its
lead prompt `cache/m5/8/lead-prompt.md`.
**Item 8 is done** (2026-10-31, merged as "Merge m5/8 (The end)"; §8): the login placement waits for the world and retries once, the Holtburg walk steps around the Pumpkin Buffer, the player build runs over four frames, the rare slow frame read as a wait outside the work; the cluster reviews, the audit table (the spec §7), section 9 at M5's end, the doc pass, M6's seed list (the spec §7.1); the gate green in one round. The hero video was cut from a fresh take (D158; §8). **M5 is complete.** Next: M6, "the level ladder", in planning (`cache/m6/lead-prompt.md`).

(Each lead appends its item's line to §8: what changed from the table, the agent size table, the gate numbers, the live
rounds, the mod installs, the spend by the meter.)

## 2. Seams and owners

One owner per file set; another owner's need goes under "For the lead" in a reply, never as an edit. Within an item,
a slice may edit only the files its §4 row names.

| `src/Lifestone.Client/Session/Grounds/**` (new: the profile records, the embedded JSON profiles, the loader) | item 2 | `HuntingGround` (immutable; a missing field fails at load); `GroundCatalog.Get(key)`; the den's constants read from it |
| `src/Lifestone.Client/Session/Autopilot/**` (new: the state machine, the checkpoint, the stop rules, the cycle line) | item 4 | `AutopilotScenario`, `AutopilotState`, `Checkpoint`, the `autopilot cycle` and `done` lines (additive fields) |

## 3. The waves

A wave is one to three items with a theme and a checkpoint; an item is one fresh lead session; a slice is one agent.
Estimates are shares of a week on the owner's meter, item and lead together, sized from M4's as-run shares (m4-plan.md
§8: a loop item with live rounds 5-6%, a panel item 3%, a mechanical item 2-4%, P5a and P5b about 5% each).

| Wave | Item | Theme and content | Gate | Share |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0 | **The measuring run and the two live bugs** (the lead, with four slices): the den's numbers (DoD 1) by a loopback measuring class and read-only SELECTs; the client-side object cull (D16) and its host side; the runner's one-waypoint fix with a runner exception made loud and Godot's `ERROR:` lines counted by smoke, slice-check and the gate; the `[soak]` metrics line and the outside loop script; `--max-fps` in the gate's Godot runs and one log instead of three; the branch `m4/hero-visual-bugs` merged (the diagnosis and `AvatarJumpWatch`) | `ObjectCullTests`, `NavRunnerTests`; `godot_errors=0` in the smoke summary (judged, a fact); the numbers in §8 | 3% |

Build 40% plus about 2% of planning: **42% against the 45% stop line (D12)**. A lead asks before exceeding its item's
share by 20% (M3 D5), and each checkpoint re-estimates the waves left against what remains.

Order: 0 → 1 → 2 → 3 → (4 and 5 in parallel) → 6 → 7 → 8. Item 1 needs item 0's numbers and its two fixes; item 2
needs item 1's caster (the tour's casts go through it); item 3 needs item 2's profile (the vendors and the kit are
profile values); item 4 needs items 1-3 (the arrows it joins); item 5 needs only item 0 (the merge of the diagnosis
branch) and runs beside 4; item 6 needs nothing above and may start when a lead is free; item 7 needs 4, 5 and 6; item
8 is last. Each later wave is sliced by its first lead at the wave boundary (M3 P8), in the shape of §4, with at most
four owner questions in one call.

## 4. Wave 0's and wave 1's slice tables

Every slice: a fresh agent (the model by D18), 60-120 calls, under 300K of context, in its own worktree cut from the
item branch `m5/<item>` (`.claude/worktrees/m5-<item>-<slice>`, `git config core.longpaths true`); at most four files
to create and eight to edit; one acceptance command (`slice-check.ps1 -MinTests <count> -Steps <steps> -Rerun`, with
`-Exhaustive` for a slice that touches `src/Lifestone.Data` or the Client folders M4 D30 names, `Movement` among
them), one Godot step at most; the handoff file `docs/handoff-m5-<item>.md` in the branch (removed before the merge).
A new judge or verdict field is report-only in the slice that adds it. The test baseline is the count `slice-check.ps1`
reports on master at the item's start (9,238 default and 736 Exhaustive at M4's end; M4 D28: a floor only the lead
lowers, for deletions it names). Rounds of one agent stay under 300K: an agent that needs a second round ends with its
handoff and a fresh agent continues.

### 4.1 Item 0 The measuring run and the two live bugs (branch `m5/0`)

**W0 (the lead; read-only plus the live measures; the backend started and stopped by the lead):**
- a loopback measuring class, run by the lead only, over BSD Mage in the den: the respawn delay (30 minutes of the
  17 slaves' deaths and re-creations), mana per cast and the regeneration at rest and in combat (the 0x02E? vital
  pushes against the session clock), the cast time from the request to the projectile and the damage for Flame Bolt
  I-VII, Flame Streak, Flame Arc and Cassius' Ring of Fire 1785 (`FightTally`), Flame Bolt VII's component burn over
  100 casts (the pack's counts before and after), the ring's kills per cast on a cluster, the north wing's caster
  damage against the buffed mage (one bounded probe from the stairs, the kill switch armed), the mage's run speed;
- read-only SELECTs of the pinned world database: the den's generator rows (`RegenerationInterval`, the links' delays),
  the tuskers' treasure profile 448 and create lists, Cindrue's and Thelnoth's whole stock with prices, the Scrivener
  of War Magic's position in 0xA9B3 and the nearest Scriveners of the other three schools;
- a level-80 mage's buffed War Magic from `MageSpendingPlanner` and the XpTable (a unit test that prints it);
- the numbers written into §8 and the spec's §5.4 and §5.7 amended where they disagree;
- the branch `m4/hero-visual-bugs` merged into `m5/0` first (the diagnosis doc and `AvatarJumpWatch`; its gate is
  `slice-check -Gate`).

### 4.3 Item 6 Retail for the person at the keys, and the two sizes (branch `m5/6`; beside item 1, D30)

Run beside item 1 (D30): until master's log shows item 1's merge, item 6 never starts or uses the backend (no live step,
no Loopback test, no `-Changed`, no `-Smoke`, no `smoke.ps1 -Backend auto`; `slice-check -Steps` takes offline steps
only), and at most one Godot process runs across its agents at a time (item 1 holds two of the machine's three). Item 6
merges second.

**The capture (the lead, before the trackers; M4 §5 item 6).** All 469 recordings under `cache\` read (the notes:
`cache/m5/6/capture.md`): every login holds, at the PlayerDescription's time and in this order, GE 0x0029
CharacterTitle (16 bytes of payload), GE 0x0021 FriendsListUpdate (an empty list) and GE 0x0295 SetTurbineChatChannels
(ten channel ids, byte-identical in all 2,664 copies, three at login and three more about 32 s later, each beside a
0x028B); the only other unknowns are GM 0x02D9 PrivateUpdatePropertyInstanceID (2,412, the current attacker) and GM
0x02DB PrivateUpdatePosition (1). No recording holds GE 0x0274 or 0x0276, any of GM 0x02D0-0x02D6, or any of the client
actions this item adds: those are specified from ACE's writers and readers and captured live in the lead's live step.
The spec's DoD 11 names 0x02D5, which ACE never sends; the four public updates ACE sends are 0x02D0, 0x02D2, 0x02D4,
0x02D6 (D31).

**Lanes.** A, the wire then its read model (w1 → w2); B, the spell bars and keys (s1); C, the camera and the two sizes
(v1); then the panels (u1 ∥ u2, after w2); then the checks (k1 ∥ k2, after u1 and u2). The contracts between lanes
(the callback names, the `IActionApi` members, the read model, the layout store) are in `cache/m5/6/lanes.md`. Merges:
the lead, in the order w1, s1, v1, w2, u1, u2, k1, k2.

Sliced by item 2's lead at the wave boundary, with D38 (auto-looting off by default, the build's one purse); item 3's lead
re-reads it at its start against item 2's "as run" and changes rows by name. Every vendor, price, threshold and target
list is a value of the ground's profile (§4.4 g1; D46: any dungeon's, the den the first), never a constant; the rows'
"the den's profile" means the ground the fight is on. At most six job items a slice.

*Changed by item 3's lead (2026-10-27, after item 2's "as run" and D48-D52):* three rows named more than eight files and
are split (m0 out of m1: the profile's supplies section; d2 out of d1: the death's grants removed; c1 into c1 the grants,
p1 the places and c2 the station and the checks); m3 and m4 read m0's supplies, not hand lists; d1's corpse trip takes
its route as a plan value (D51); the purse is coin and trade notes (D49); the first tie moves into the build (D50); the
gate's trip is forced (D52). Every row reads the ground of the fight (D46), and both dungeon profiles are under test.

### 4.6 Item 4 The outer loop and the dial (branch `m5/4`; wave 3, beside item 5 under D54)

Sliced by item 4's lead at its start (2026-10-28), after item 3's "as run" and D54; D55-D58 settle what item 3 left open.
The loop is one state machine over the journey's own stations (D57): the autopilot adds the arrows, the checkpoint, the
reconnect, the stop rules and the lines, never a second copy of a station. Every den-specific value stays a value of
the ground's profile (D46); the autopilot's own limits (the watchdogs, the retries) are Lifestone's own and named as such.
Re-placed at the start: `m5-avoid-creature-reach` (M6, with the retreat rule of `m5-fight-death-midrun`; the loop handles
the death itself), 2-R14 `m5-2-leg-logic-copies` (the autopilot reaches the legs only through the stations; the copies
stay for the end item), 3-R12 and 3-R17 (the next slice that edits the file).

**Lanes.** a1 and c2 together; after a1: a2, h1 and h2 (at most three agents and two Godot runners at once, D54); a3
after a2; h3 after h2 and h1; c1 after a3, h1 and c2; then the doc pass, the review and the fix slice. c2, h1 (the login
check's hook), h3 and c1 touch D54's shared files and are named to item 5's lead before they start.

### 4.7 Item 5 A solid world (branch `m5/5`; wave 3, beside item 4 under D54)

Sliced by item 5's lead at its start (2026-10-28), after item 3's "as run" and D54. The physics is built in
`Lifestone.Data` (the transitions, the shadows, retail's object-collision rules, doors' BSPs, `SetEthereal`); what is
missing is the host that puts the server's objects into the player's `PhysicsWorld` and the avatars' step through it
(docs/milestones/m4-hero-visual-bugs.md §4; D70-D72). Every row is any dungeon's and any town's (D46): no den, Holtburg
or Renald fact is a constant; a door, a lifestone and a creature are what their Setup and PhysicsState say. Godot: one
runner of the lead's at a time (D54); a lane that would run a Godot step asks the lead first.

**Lanes.** h1 alone; then t1, d1 and h2 together (h2's player rig and the headless body, t1's avatar step, d1's
wayfinder and stations: no file shared); l1 after h2; c1 after item 4's c2 merges (tools/**, D54 rule 3) and after d1;
then the doc pass, the review and the fix slice. At most three agents at once.

| Slice | Scope | Files (create / edit) | Acceptance | Size |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| h1 the server-object physics host | `ServerObjectPhysicsHost` (src/Lifestone.Client/Physics): one `PhysicsObj` in the player's `PhysicsWorld` for every server object whose Setup has collision (cylinders, spheres, parts with physics BSPs: doors, lifestones, NPCs, creatures), made on create from its Setup and scale (Setups cached by id), moved with `ForceIntoCell` when its position or cell changes (`RecalcCrossCells` on demand), its state from the object's PhysicsState (Ethereal: an open door; Hidden; IgnoreCollisions), its weenie flags for retail's rules (a creature is never stepped onto, players pass players), removed on delete, pickup, parenting and leaving the world, a landblock not yet attached handled as `PhysicsWorld` does (the lost cell); fed through a small feed interface the avatar set calls (create, move, state, remove), so the headless body can feed it from the object table (h2); `ServerObjectPhysicsTests` on the dats: a closed door stops `OfflineWalker`, the same door Ethereal lets it through, a lifestone's cylinder stops it, an NPC's cylinder moves with the NPC, 0 bytes for a standing crowd's updates | create `Physics/ServerObjectPhysicsHost.cs`, `Physics/IServerObjectPhysics.cs` (the feed), `ServerObjectPhysicsTests.cs`; edit `Avatars/AvatarSet.cs` (the feed calls), `PhysicsObj` only additively (D71) | `slice-check -MinTests <base> -Exhaustive` (no Godot step) | Opus high, ~110 |
| t1 the transitions for server objects (D45; after h1) | the avatar body's step (`AvatarBody.UpdateObjectInternal`, the "Retail's transition runs here" site) runs retail's transition through its host object (`CPhysicsObj::transition` and `SetPositionInternal(transit)`, cited) and takes its result (cell, frame, contact plane), so creatures collide with each other and with the player; the ground clamp (`TryGroundZ`) retired for stepped objects; contact and walkable per D72; the sticky press and the interpolation's progress checks against the collided spots; the cost: transition time per frame in `AvatarStats` (no allocation), a standing and a walking crowd at 0 bytes, and an excess made cheaper here (D45), never cut; `ServerObjectPhysicsTests`: two creatures pressed to one point end apart; `AvatarJumpWatchTests`' stuck creature no longer snaps (the fixed case pinned, the case without the host kept) | edit `Avatars/AvatarBody.cs`, `Avatars/AvatarSet.cs`, `Physics/ServerObjectPhysicsHost.cs`, `AvatarJumpWatchTests.cs`, `ServerObjectPhysicsTests.cs`, the avatars' allocation tests | `slice-check -MinTests <base> -Exhaustive` (no Godot step) | Opus high, ~120 |

for M6; `m5-offline-walkcheck-rate` stays item 7's (the soak's fixes).

## 5. Gates and the live-gate rules

M4's §5 in full, with its P4, P5b and fresh-gate changes (the four-lane parallel gate, at most three Godot windows,
the journeys judged by their done lines, G-A as `slice-check -Gate`, the offline Godot smoke once per item, fresh
journey characters each gate), and these M5 changes:

1. **Branches and worktrees:** `m5/<item>`, `.claude/worktrees/m5-<item>-<slice>`; the merged item's worktrees and
   branches removed after its merge (M4 D15, D21 here).
2. **The checkpoint after every wave (D17):** the wave's last lead writes the spend per item against §3, the DoD items
   met so far, the measured rates, the frames, and pings the owner; nothing is cut or started until the owner answers.
3. **New verdict fields and their first gates:** item 0's `objects= in_cell= light_rows=` report-only; `godot_errors`
   judged 0 from its first gate (a fact, not a threshold: the named exception, as M4 D31's audio fact); item 1's
   `mana_tries= mana_landed= busy= resists= proved= mana_wait_max_s= kills_per_hour=` report-only, `mana_wait_max_s<=10`
   judged from item 2's gate; item 2's `storeys_visited= basement_entries=` report-only, judged from item 3's gate;
   item 3's cycle fields report-only, `granted=0 places=0` judged from item 4's gate; item 4's `cycles= reconnects=
   interrupted= resumed=` report-only, `cycles>=2` judged from item 6's gate; item 5's `door_opened=` report-only,
   judged from item 7's gate; the soak's verdicts report-only (one soak). Item 7's new fields are report-only in their first gate: `openers=` (F8), `picks=` (F10), `defended=` (F4), `revitalizes= stamina_min=` (F5), `drains= heals= health_min=` (F7).
4. **The autopilot lane (D19):** in the `test` lane after the mage's journey (one character per account in the world),
   `smoke -Only autopilot-mage -ContendedFrames`: 30 minutes of the autopilot on BSD Mage with one trip forced early
   (`--autopilot-trip-after-kills=5`) and one scripted death (M4 D37, `--autopilot-test-death`), judged by its done
   line as the journeys are (`Get-AutopilotVerdict`); its frames report-only (contended). The three journeys and the
   tools lane stay as they are.
5. **The two sizes (D9):** the gate's offline step runs `smoke.ps1 -AllSizes` for the shell frames and the play-checks
   at 1280x720 and 1920x1080 from item 6 on; the frame judge stays at 800x600 (M2 D57).
6. **The soak (D6, D23, D54):** once, in item 7, alone on the machine for one night, the backend under the lead for the night, the Godot
   play host at 1280x720 with `--max-fps=60`, recording off, the `[soak]` line and `tools/soak-watch.ps1`, a frame
   every 30 min for the owner's eye (checked for held items and models before it is shown); at most one re-run after a
   fix, then the numbers recorded and the owner asked.
7. **Rounds, not marathons:** at most three live rounds per item, then the lead stops, writes the numbers into §8 and
   asks the owner (M4 §5 item 7).
8. **The owner's eye on every frame** (M4 §5 item 8): the lead checks the held items and the models before sending,
   says plainly what is a placeholder, and now also reads the `AvatarJumpWatch` counts of a den frame.

## 6. The rules and the process

The rules are CLAUDE.md, "Hard rules" (fidelity under "Quality") and "Rules for agent sessions"; a brief cites them
and restates nothing. The templates in `cache/m5-prompt-templates.md` (gitignored; M4's T1-T7 with the names changed,
made by item 0's lead from `cache/m4-prompt-templates.md`) carry a rules block whose lines must equal CLAUDE.md's; a
lead compares the two at the start of its item and corrects the template. The process decisions are M3's P1-P8
(m3-plan.md §6) with M4's amendments (the checkpoint after every wave, a planned research phase per item, the roadmap
line at every transition, frames judged solo) and D18's model mix. One rule is M5's own, from the spec's §4: a
den-specific fact is a profile value, and a lead refuses a slice that adds one as a constant.

Templates: **T1** the item lead prompt (one fresh session per item, with its settings table: the model, the effort,
the mode, the worktree, the subagent limits, the budget share and ask-line); **T2** the implementer slice brief; **T3**
the parallel lane brief; **T4** the review brief; **T5** the fix-slice brief; **T6** the merge brief; **T7** the
live-gate runbook, with the soak's runbook added in item 7. Each lead writes the next item's T1 into `cache/` before it
ends.

## 7. Decisions

D1-D11 are the owner's, given in the M5 workshop of 2026-10-25 (recorded, not quoted); D12-D24 are the planner's
defaults, which the owner may overrule at any checkpoint.

| # | Question | Blocks | Decided |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | The direction after M4 | everything | **Single player first; patrons later.** The product is a game that plays itself from a character's start to the game's end, with a lot of grinding, where the person plays as much or as little as they like, by hand or watching. Not a demo: the game. M5 moves the project from writing retail to "retail+" |
| D2 | Which class first | the autopilot, the profiles, the gate | **Mages, broadly; the other classes from M6 on.** A mage is the character that automates fully with vendor gear and Item Magic; the fighters' journeys stay as the gate's coverage of melee and missile |
| D3 | The milestone's shape | §3 | **M5 is the first dungeon; M6 adds dungeons and regions to idle in, rapidly, as data** |
| D4 | The whole dungeon, or M4 D12's arrival floor | item 2 | **Capable of the whole den; in practice never the drudge and virindi wing** (not worth the time). D12 is superseded by the profile's avoid rule |
| D5 | The autopilot's character | items 3-4, the soak | **The test character, BSD Mage, for now** (re-created at each gate under M4 D44; the soak runs on the gate's copy); a character of the owner's own is a later decision |
| D6 | The backend up for days | the soak | **Yes**, under the lead |
| D7 | The server's `xp_modifier` | the grind's numbers | **No; retail rates.** Players grinded one dungeon for a month at several points in the game's history; a month in the den is the plan |
| D8 | The components Holtburg does not sell (Platinum, Pyreal and Gold Scarabs), and the foci | item 3 | **The owner's point: retail's mages carried the four foci**, so every cast burns only its scarab and Prismatic Tapers (the spec §5.7). The kit holds the four foci and a first stack of Platinum and Pyreal Scarabs, granted by the build (the cheat layer) until the mage can buy them; a stock-up trip to a Scrivener or Archmage Philomon is a profile step for a later item; a stack under three stops the loop loudly |
| D9 | The window | item 6, the soak | **It is resizable already; fix the camera's field of view on a resize (`cam-fov-resize`) and test 1280x720 and 1920x1080**; the soak at 1280x720 |
| D10 | The layers above the first dungeon | §3.2 | **Recorded, deferred** (the spec §5.1): the goal planner and the quest layer, the service bots of retail (buff, tinker, dye, ingot bots on their own accounts, the companion design as their base), the self-levelling alts with a trade-skill focus, the crafting bots |
| D11 | The knowledge corpus | items 2, 3, 8; M6 | **ACOracle, the owner's sibling checkout, is the source** for grounds by level, quest profiles, recipes, vendors and the service-bot customs, under the spec §5.10's rules; nothing of it is committed to Lifestone |
| D12 | The budget | every item | **40% build plus about 2% planning against a 45% stop line**, in shares of the weekly meter (M4 D2's shape); a lead asks before exceeding its item's share by 20%; the meter resets 2026-10-26 |
| D13 | Every automated cast as `ActionSource.Automation` | item 1 | **Yes** (the P5b design's Q11): the kill switch and dry run then reach buff passes, keeper casts and recalls, as automation-vision R6 says |
| D14 | The fizzle rule | item 1 | **The same level twice more, then one level down once, then a named miss** for the buff lines and the bolt (the design's Q2); recalls, ties and the keeper keep their bounds; the owner asked for all of the design's fixes |
| D15 | Keep pressing cast short of mana | item 1 | **Yes, by default:** at most 8 tries a need, 0.25 s apart, from a 25%-a-try floor, every try counted; `--cast-mana-tries 0` restores today's ceiling (the design's Q3) |
| D16 | The client-side cull | item 0 | **Ported from retail's rule** (the 25 s cull ACE's `ObjectMaint` relies on; the retail function named where the symbol table gives it); never the player, an inventory item or an open container; the interim log-off is not used |
| D17 | The checkpoint and the cut line | every wave | **A checkpoint after every wave** (M4 D3's shape); the cut order if the owner cuts: item 6, then item 5's transitions part, then item 2's third profile (item 5's transitions taken off the cut order by D45) |
| D18 | The model mix | every item | **M4's** (m4-plan.md D38's mix: Opus 5.5 at high effort for the per-item lead, the item review and the design-heavy slices named in §4; Sonnet 5.5 for the other slices, lanes and fixes; Haiku 5.5 for merges without conflicts, doc passes and lookups); the owner may name another model for a lead at the item's start |
| D19 | The gate | items 4-7 | **The autopilot lane in the `test` lane after the mage's journey** (30 min, one early trip, one scripted death), report-only in its first gate; `godot_errors=0` judged from its first gate as a fact; the soak once |
| D20 | The autopilot's key | item 4 | **One retail's default map leaves unbound**, picked by item 4's lead from the key map in the dats (docs/retail-ui.md §22.1), beside `Pause` (auto-buff), `F7` (tools) and `Scroll Lock` (the inspector); a panel button does the same |
| D21 | Worktrees and branches | every item | `m5/<item>`; the worktrees and branches of merged items removed after each merge (M4 D15); `m4/hero-visual-bugs` merged in item 0; the other leftover M4 worktrees left alone (CLAUDE.md) |

(3) Shared files (`Main.cs`, `scripts/World/**`, `scripts/Session/*Host.cs`, the stand-in and the play-checks, `tools/**`, `tests/Shared/**`): one slice at a time across both items, named to the other lead before it starts; `OptionCatalog` append only. Headless walks therefore meet doors, lifestones and creatures as statics that move, while the avatars' own transitions (t1) run only where avatars are stepped (the game) |

| D73 | A create around the player (item 5's lead, from F1) | item 5 | **A server object is created where the server puts it, and the player is moved out of it**: the host places a new object with retail's sliding `enter_world` (11.6096 0x00516C70) against everything but the player, then the player's body takes the same sliding placement (`OfflineWalker.ReplaceAfterObjectsEntered`, each frame in the game, each frame headless). Retail reaches this order by placing the player only after the destination's objects exist (`SmartBox::TeleportPlayer`, 0x004539B0); Lifestone places the player first, so sliding the object instead would leave a lifestone's collision 2.2 m from its drawn stone. A create that fits nowhere is forced in, counted and logged (`TODO(m5-create-forced)`) |
| D74 | The interpolation at retail's rate (item 5's lead, from the live gate's round 2 and F7) | item 5 | **The interpolation's progress check counts update steps at retail's rate**: retail's `InterpolationManager::adjust_offset` (11.6096 0x00556950) bumps `frame_counter` once per object update, and objects update only behind `CPhysics::UseTime`'s 30 Hz gate (0x0050A420). Lifestone steps the avatars every drawn frame, so at about 240 Hz the 5-frame check ran eight times too often and a creature held back 0.1 s by another was blipped (round 2's den fight: 60 RootJumps in 75 s, most `placed=Blip blocked_by=creature`). A frame now counts per 1/30 s of quantum (`UpdateInterval`); the drawn step stays per frame. This restores retail's timing, not a glitch, so no flag; `UpdateInterval = 0` keeps the old count for the measure. Offline, 16 tuskers on a standing player: 516 blips a minute at 240 Hz before, 256 after (260 at 30 Hz) |
| D85 | Checkpoint 3 (the owner, 2026-10-28) | items 7, 8 | **Go: item 7 starts now, as planned, inside one 12-hour window from its start** (2026-10-28, about 11:05 UTC to 23:00 UTC: the machine's time the owner gives the item), so the soak is a run of hours, not a night. Inside the window: the long-run fixes cut to what an unattended run needs, G-A, the live gate and a rehearsal of about 30 minutes; then the soak from about 16:00 UTC to 21:00 UTC at the latest (at least four hours; a fix not done by 16:00 UTC is a named gap, never a reason to start later); then the backend stopped, the verdicts read, the item-end check, the merge and the write-up by 23:00 UTC. DoD 13 reads its verdicts over that run, report-only as D54 has it. The owner's hand-off by the keys (`m5-autopilot-lead-handoff`) is taken whenever the owner is at the machine during the soak (a movement key takes over, Num Lock hands back, the soak's log counts it); the soak never waits for it. `m5-den-creature-blips` was not answered: retail's rule stays for item 7, and the row stays the owner's for item 8 |
| D86 | The soak's end (the owner, 2026-10-28, relayed during item 7; amends D85's end) | item 7 | **The soak runs until the owner needs the machine, at most 24 hours**: its own stop rule is 24 hours from its start (`tools/soak.ps1 -Minutes 1440`), so it ends cleanly if nobody stops it, and 21:00 UTC is no longer its end. The stop is the owner's word (relayed or direct); on it, within minutes, the lead closes the game window (the game logs off and the autopilot writes its checkpoint with the totals; no `autopilot done` line then, so the report reads the cycle lines), stops the backend and deletes the lock; the machine is then the owner's. After an owner's stop the verdicts and the docs are written at once with no Godot, and the item-end check (`slice-check -Changed -AllSizes`, which opens game windows) and the merge wait for the owner's word that the machine is free; if the 24-hour rule ends the soak, they follow at once. Over the soak the lead looks in every 30 to 60 minutes, not every 5 |

| D100 | The creature buffs' orbs and the cast gestures' hand sparks share a particle emitter id (the owner, 2026-10-28, on a side item after a live gate) | item 8 (the hero video) | **Retail's rule stays: no glitch fix, no flag.** Every AttribUp, SkillUp, HealthUp and RegenUp script of the human table makes its one emitter as id 1, and every talisman cast gesture makes its hand sparks as id 1 at its first frame (e.g. animation 0x03000596). Retail's `ParticleManager::CreateParticleEmitter` (11.6096 0x0051C1F0) destroys the emitter holding an id before it makes the new one, so the next cast removes the orbs. When casts are spaced as they were in the owner's era the orbs play out; at the late-retail buffing pace, which the autopilot's 1.4 s cast pace matches, they are cut short. That is how the game behaved, so it is not a glitch, and the autopilot's pace is not changed. `BuffEmitterIdTests` pins retail's behaviour on the real data. The life shells (ShieldUp, allocated ids) were a separate question, checked live: neither effect reached the player at all, because the client sent self buffs untargeted (`m5-buff-effects-drawn`, closed 2026-10-31 below). With that fixed, a skill buff's orbs show for about a second before the next cast's gesture removes them, an attribute buff's (which fade in) barely, and the shells play out |

The stop (135.6, 15.8), chosen by item 3's F4 as the floor polygon nearest him, lies among three barrels (Setup 0x020000A5, cylinders of 0.27 m: two on the floor at (134.83, 16.05) and (135.75, 16.05), one stacked) against the north wall beside his counter (0x02000272, its west face at x 135.14): 0.29 m from a barrel's axis, where the human's 0.48 m foot sphere fits nowhere. The pre-check is right (retail's `CTransition::step_down`, 11.6096 0x0050BD70, finds no walkable there either; the route's cell, the door crossing and the floor polygons are all as the dats have them), so the fix is the profile value, not the physics: the new point is where the walks of M4 P5b and M5 item 0 ended and sold (F1's floor arrival is 0.2 m from it), its 0.5 m stop ring stands on the floor through the statics, it is 6.4 m from Renald (5.0 m cylinder to cylinder, his use radius), and ACE's move-to from any point of the ring arrives in under a second on the floor. The route test's clear-floor check read floor polygons only and passed the barrels; a stop point is now also proved by the walker's own pre-check and the stop ring through the statics (`VendorStopConfirmDatTests`, Exhaustive: the den's three vendors, the old point as the refusal it was, Monyra and Ecutha, Holtburg's two vendors no profile names: Monyra's own point confirms; Ecutha's leg is refused in her lobby 0xA9B40127, where the room graph's doorway-to-doorway line passes a pillar (static 0x0200020E), a named gap under `nav-static-footprint` until a profile names her). A Holtburg fact stays a profile value; no constant moved. **The second case (round 10, 03:25 UTC) is a different fault with the same face: a same-cell vendor's own point.** Thelnoth Cort the Healer was not yet in the object table when the walk was planned (the mage stood in Cindrue's shop), so the station sent its point request to his own point with the 0.5 m stop tolerance; the plan passed the pre-check (F3: the fixed world only, by design), the mage walked through his door, and jammed about a metre from the point, 19 rungs over 3 plans, stuck. Since item 5 (D70) his body stands in the player's physics world, so the point under him is never reached; before item 5 the same request arrived (item 4's gate, 2026-10-28 06:12 UTC). The fix: an unseen same-cell vendor's point request carries `ShopLeg.UnseenVendorReachMetres` (1.6 m: the model's default use radius 0.6 plus the close rule's margin 1.0, inside every vendor's reach; the Holtburg vendors' UseRadius is 3 m, Renald's 5), so the walk stops short of his body and the open finds him in reach; a vendor already in view keeps his object goal at his use radius, and a front-room stop point keeps no range (the body stands on it). Reproduced and proved on the dats with his body hosted in his cellar (`VendorStopConfirmDatTests`: the 0.5 m goal ends stuck 0.96 m from his point; the 1.6 m goal arrives in 0.4 s, 0.23 m cylinder to cylinder) and in `ResupplyStationTests` |

| D141 | Check before, act, check after, on every automated action (the owner, 2026-10-30 about 03:40 UTC, relayed) | item 7 | **The owner's point: the failures come from making assumptions.** (1) BEFORE: read the game state for everything the action needs; fix a missing precondition first (wield a caster, free a slot, get mana) or fail at once with a plain named reason; never send on an assumption. (2) ACT. (3) AFTER: read the state to see the result happened (the stance is magic, the item is in the hand or the pack, the coin changed, the target is dead or gone); "confirmed" or "no error" is not proof; a result the server turned into something else (peace for magic) fails at once with the reason, no timeout. (4) On a failure a named recovery fixes the precondition and retries once, else stops safely with the reason logged. **Where (the lead):** in the action layer, once for every caller: each action's tracker already owns a before-check (`Refusal`, on the client's state) and an after-check (its confirmation); D141 makes the before-check name the missing precondition as a typed reason, makes the after-check fail fast on a contrary result, and makes the client's state match the server after a refusal (round 13's root: after ACE refused the held Wand's move, the client still believed the Wand held, so the stance check passed on a false state). One recovery table in the autopilot maps a typed reason to its fix and one retry. **Slices:** P7a carries the stance, wield and pack moves (the model case); P7e sweeps every other tracker. |

| D145 | The bot is controlled from the chat window: /stop and /auto (the owner, 2026-10-30 about 07:40 UTC, relayed after trip check 4: for now the bot is started and stopped only by /stop and /auto, to be made more robust in the next milestone; the bot carries on and locks the WASD controls out for the player until the player types /stop, after which the game is fully player driven; /auto makes the bot evaluate every state a fight needs, then resume as best it can; implement that, then do the long test; also double-check the hand-over of control and the hand-back) | item 7 | **Two typed controls, handled by the client and never sent to the server** (ACE has no `stop` or `auto` command; its `stop-now` is another name). Num Lock no longer toggles the autopilot, and D98's key hand-off (a person's movement key takes the controls) is retired. **While the bot runs, the movement keys do nothing** (forward, back, turns, strafes, jump, run/walk): a rate-limited chat line says the autopilot has the controls and /stop takes them. Chat, camera, mouse, windows and inspection stay free. Anything else the person does is for the bot's own checks to put right (D141). **/stop stops him where he stands**: no recall, no log off. It ends the current action, cancels the queue, releases every held key and unlocks the keys. Any stop the bot makes on its own (an error, stop=failed, a give-up) unlocks them the same way, with a chat line saying why: a stopped bot never locks the person out. **/auto re-reads every state a fight needs from the live state, not from memory** (D141): alive or dead, portal space, where he is, the caster held, the stance, each buff line and its time left, the vitals, the packs, burden, supplies and components, an open vendor, corpse or trade, hostiles and attackers, the loot queue, and whether a trip is due. It fixes each gap with its named recovery, logs the whole audit, puts a one-line summary in chat, and resumes from where he is, with every walk named (D47); from somewhere with no route, it says so and recovers the usual way (recall, then the way back). The soak harness's stop file stays the run's own end. **Proved live before the long run, with frames:** W during a fight does nothing and shows the line; /stop mid-fight stops him in place and W then moves him; from a bad state (walked off the spot, peace stance, caster put in a pack) /auto puts each right, says so and resumes; /stop and /auto once on a town trip. **Then the long run** on that build (the owner's choice over the fresh gate), as round 13: recorded, the 20-minute check to the relay, the display held on. It also shows Spirit Drinker's 30-minute recast and an honest Flame Bolt figure on the Sceptre alone: each Flame Bolt hit logs the held caster and whether the aura is up (round 13's 178.8 mixed the Wand, 19:13-21:13 UTC, with the Sceptre; ACE's math gives +3.9% on the Sceptre, 1.03 to 1.07). Making the two commands more robust is the next milestone's (section 9, `m5-7-chat-controls-robust`). |

| D151 | Diagnose the login pause first, then finish (the owner, 2026-10-31 12:18 UTC, relayed: back to the morning choice, option 2, and get this finished) | item 7 | **The cause is the GC's dynamic heap count (DATAS), not the night's code and not the allocation.** The shell build allocated the same 18-22 MiB as in every earlier gate. The collection that closes its no-GC window ran on too few threads and took 75 and 135 ms at the item-end rerun (10-22 ms before), with little CPU for its length. On a fresh backend it still happened: 3 of 8 logins over 50 ms (107.8 and 78.8 ms collections, one 74 ms wait). **Fix:** the Godot process runs a fixed 8 server-GC heaps (`DOTNET_GCHeapCount=8` from `tools\godot.ps1`, DATAS off). Over 8 logins: that collection 7-22 ms, every login at most 39 ms, and the login's GC pause in all 11-31 ms against 55-221 ms, for about 150 MB more managed memory. The slow-frame line now names its render, physics and process-CPU shares, and the closing line its CPU (docs/game-core.md §7). **Not fixed here:** a rarer wait of about 75-80 ms with no GC, no drawing and little CPU (the 2026-10-31 01:55 and 03:28 UTC gates, the 12:25 UTC baseline, one of 17 logins today). It is older than the night's code and stays item 8's `m5-8-login-walk-and-frames`. **Proof:** the three login-check runs alone, under the owner's rule (merge only on green). |

| D153 | Fix the stuck walk, rerun, merge (the owner, 2026-10-31 13:13 UTC, relayed: fix, rerun, merge) | item 7 | **The cause:** `RouteDriver` (the walker of the login check, the death approach and the frames runner, older than this item's steering) remembered the keys it pressed and never checked them. At 13:04:49 UTC (`cache/gate/d152-r2/frame-judge-1`) every held key was let go about 0.7 s into the Academy exit: `keys(substates=0)`, the run key up, forward Ready, the server not in control. That is `CommandInterpreter::LoseKeyboardFocus`, very likely the window's focus going to the owner's click in another app (focus changes were not logged). The walker stood still 2.95 m short of goal 0. **Fix (D141):** each frame the driver asks the controller (`IPlayerMovementIntents.IsHeld`) and presses a lost key again with the run key, logged once each time (`KeysPressedAgain`). The window's focus out and in are logged. Test: `RouteDriverTests.M5_7_D153_...` (the keys dropped mid-walk, the walk finishes). Then the suite, the login checks 3 runs each alone, and the merge only if all are clean. |
| D154 | Fix the stale kit check, rerun, merge (the owner, 2026-10-31 13:55 UTC, relayed: fix, rerun, merge) | item 7 | **The item-end check (`slice-check -Changed -AllSizes`, 13:26-13:52 UTC) failed 3 steps from one offline play-check:** `a-stand-in-gear-kit-arrives-in-the-pack-with-every-line-held-as-a-world-object` read `kit=17/17 requested=21`. The kit arrived whole, and the second spawn asked for nothing. The check's sum was stale: since F3 (D87, 2026-10-28) a component line wider than one stack is asked for in several requests. It last passed on 2026-10-28 07:25 UTC with the 11-line kit, and no item 7 lane ran it after. record-audio shares that run, and shell-frames logs the same line. **Fix:** the check adds the lines requested (a line counted once) to the lines already held, and its detail names `lines_requested=` and `requests=`. Then the suite and the item-end check again; the merge only if all green. |

No decision recorded the switch, so D38's "the soak runs with it off" is amended here: the long run looted and sold (452 items sold in the run of 2026-10-30). |

## 8. As run

**Planning (2026-10-25).** One Fable 5.1 session in the owner's M5 workshop, read-only until the docs were written, on
master bfe466b7 while M4's last commit was being made. The direction came in the workshop (D1-D11). Five read-only
audit agents ran in parallel (their notes gitignored under `cache/m5/`; the synthesis `cache/m5-gap-analysis.md`):
the protocol and game-system coverage (Opus, 92 calls, 285K tokens), the retail UI coverage (Sonnet, 101 calls, 330K),
the days-long run risks (Opus, 173 calls, 363K), the cheat ledger and the mage's economy (Sonnet, 137 calls, 497K), the
den fight loop (Sonnet, 157 calls, 523K). The XP table was read from the owner's dat by a scratch probe outside the
repository. The knowledge corpus (D11) was found in the workshop and read for the foci, the scarabs and the Scriveners.
The spend is the owner's meter reading at the session's end.

**Item 0, the measuring run and the two live bugs (2026-10-25, branch `m5/0`; one Opus 5.5 lead at high effort).**
*Changed from §4.1:* c2 was split in two: c2 kept the runner's one-waypoint route and gained the leg guard
(`LegFaultGuard` in eight Godot runners, more than eight edits, each a few lines); a new lane c5 took the checks' Godot error
count (`smoke.ps1`, `slice-check.ps1`, `live-gate.ps1`, `GateJudge.psm1`) and the gate's `--max-fps=60` (the lead's
decision: the journeys capped, the solo frame judge, the throwaway's frames and the leak scan not, since
`LoginCheckRunner.HoltburgMinimumFps` is 60). c3's "one log" is `godot.log` off by default with the console kept (every
check reads the console; `--log-copies=console|file|all`). The review's 0-R1 (the cull inside a dungeon counted the whole
landblock as visible) was fixed in this item, not left to item 2: the cull reads each EnvCell's visible-cell list from the
dat in both hosts, as ACE's `ObjectMaint.GetVisibleObjects` does. The measuring class gained ManaDance (D27) on the owner's
word. The `m4/hero-visual-bugs` merge raised the test floor 9,238 to 9,243, the lanes to 9,322, the fixes to 9,337.
*Retail's cull rule* is ACE's: `CObjectMaint::AddObjectToBeDestroyed` (11.6096 0x00509A40) queues an object for 25 s,
`UseTime` (0x00509480) deletes it when due, `RemoveObjectToBeDestroyed` (0x00508F10) cancels it; it is queued when its
cell is released (`ReleaseObjCell` 0x005086E0) or it goes to the lost cell (`GotoLostCell` 0x00508CE0). The owner's
question 3 was not needed.
*Agents* (calls, peak context): c1 Opus high 82 and 39 (the 0-R1 round), 211K and 264K; c2 Sonnet 59, 175K; c3 Sonnet 86,
243K; c4 Sonnet 101, 346K, then four resumed rounds of 6-17 calls each up to 425K (ran long: the measures' fixes, RingCluster's
and SpellTable's redesigns and ManaDance were added to its context instead of a fresh agent's); c5 Sonnet 94, 262K (one resume:
its first hand-back came before its test run ended); the review Opus high 73, 265K (0 blockers, 6 should-fix, 7 nits); the fix
slice Sonnet 60, 179K; the doc pass Haiku 33, 129K.

= 0`). Commit e2a260b1 (K3's D14) lacks its Co-Authored-By line (not rewritten).
*A correction to item 0's arithmetic:* a landing gives about 1.75 mana per point of stamina spent (158 stamina gave 277
mana in item 0, 276 in this item's VI read), not 0.875 a point, so the dance's supply in magic stance is about 0.44 +
0.66 x 1.75, 1.6 mana a second: the fight is about cast-bound even at Flame Bolt VII (about 230 kills an hour of mana at
25 a kill), and item 0's "about 145 at VII" is low.

the spec's §5.11), D46 (the project will add a great many dungeons this way: nothing built here is the den's), and D47
(watching round 2's fight pace between two rooms: the character always moves with purpose). The owner was asked once,
at the 7.2% ask line (about 1% more to finish), and said to finish it.
*Changed from §4.4:* after D46, g2b (the ground through the seams) and g7 (the second dungeon) were added and g2, g5 and
g6 made generic; the lead added g8 (the builder's town half: the arrival from the entry portal, the nearest lifestones,
the vendors with their prices within 600 m, the Use and Death emote exits, the band from the targets) and split k1 before
it started (k1 the checks, k2 the journeys). g2 corrected D39 (0x010E lies outside the wing; 13 more cells are cut off, so
the ramp walk runs 0x026E to 0x0241 and 0x023F to 0x01D3). D46 holds: Tusker Temple (0x5C4A,
`tusker-temple-fort-tethana`, home Fort Tethana) was drafted and written as one JSON file and its checks with no source
edit in about two hours; with g8's town half a new dungeon takes about 40 minutes. The den as built: 499 walkable cells
over six storeys (158, 81, 23, 148, 88 and 1), 74 no-go, 13 cut off; 117 slots (78 hunted, 5 guards in no-go cells, 34
avoided). The indoor runner: a den leg turns 0.127 to 0.136 times a metre (0.670 before; the tightest 0.248, the limit
0.25). The review (Opus high: 0 blockers, 7 should-fix, 9 nits) went to F1 (2-R1 to 2-R4 and 2-R9: kills paired by spawn
guid with the death-cell fallback, `--fight-storeys-min` steering the pick, the arrival storey's hold, the recovered legs
and unpaired kills on the verdict line) and F2 (2-R5 to 2-R7 and 2-R16), both in their own worktrees because the live
read ran off the item's. The live reads added F3 (the walk's clear line had defaulted to true, so bolts went through rock
at other storeys' tuskers; the review frames wait for a storey other than the arrival's), F4 (the schedule seeded from
the creatures standing at the fight's start: round 1's `kills_unpaired=2` of 3), F5 (D47: seen-empty slots from the
visible cells, a named purpose for every walk, the hold, the loop guard, per-leg reports) and F6 (a doorway between two
rooms is run, not walked: the runner had walked within 3 m of every cell portal, 94% of a den leg, at 2.6 m/s; offline
after F6 4% and 6.0 m/s, Tusker Temple 97% to 10%). The nits 2-R10 to 2-R14 are §9 rows.

without asking; neither was needed. *The agents* (calls and peak tokens): h1 Opus 93, 261K; t1 Opus 87, 263K; h2

server-object physics host (D70: one `PhysicsObj` per server object with collision in the player's world, fed by the
avatar set in the game and the object table headless; `PhysicsObj` additive only, D71); the creatures' transitions

558cc6f2 10,420 and 762 Exhaustive; on a1330371 10,431 and 767; on 47d57d87 10,444 and 775. *The live gate* (the
item worktree's `tools\live-gate.ps1`, the full parallel gate with the mage and melee journeys solo, fresh
characters): round 1 (558cc6f2) failed the archer's hunt and vendors, the mage's den fight, logistics, death and

**every step passed**: the mage 12 of 12 (`granted=0 places=0`; the den fight `storeys_visited=2 basement_entries=0
mana_wait_max_s=0`, 3 kills in 108 s; recall-home `stepped_off=no`), the melee 9 of 9 (`vendor` and `vendor-facts`

archer 10 of 10, the frame judge 3 of 3 clean in each group, `godot_errors=0`, secrets 0. *The jump detector*
(`AvatarJumpWatch`, the mage's den fight): before item 5 (item 3's round 4) 28 RootJumps in 81 s, 22 of them inside
the slow capture frames and 6 on normal frames (median 1.7 m, one of 28 m), with three Tusker Slaves on one spot;

The den frames show every tusker on its own spot (D45 met). The snaps are retail's rule at work: a tusker held by
another tusker on its way to the server's position falls behind and the interpolation blips it (retail's client
collides remote creatures as Lifestone now does; ACE's port sets no IgnoreCreatures for them either); the server's
spot was free at the blip (`from_server_m` 0.00 for most), so the client's and the server's creatures disagree by
latency, not by overlap. They are fewer a second than round 2's but larger and more visible than the stuck-snap of

  a Virindi Servant's corpse retried beyond reach, whose Use let ACE's own MoveTo walk the mage into the cell, which our
  no-go map cannot stop. Each recovered by recall in about 75 s (`m5-8-loot-no-go-corpse`). A Virindi shooting from
  inside that cell at 19:42 is a smaller, separate point (`m5-8-no-go-attacker`).
- **One alarm,** 20:48 UTC, "5 trips in 30 minutes": after the 20:42 sell trip the load stayed at 90%, over the 85%
  line, because kept "worth a look" items filled the review sack and the main pack. Four empty trips; the trip-loop guard
  held trips back for 30 kills at 20:46:17; no empty-trip loop after the hand-off (`m5-8-review-pack-load`).
- **The owner's live hand-off (D145).** 21:10:20 W and TurnRight swallowed, with the notice; 21:10:26 /stop mid-fight:
  stopped in place, keys unlocked. The owner recalled at 21:11:16, changed gear, put the robe back on at 21:17:21 and
  dropped 8 heavy pieces in Holtburg (never picked up again). 21:17:35 /auto: the audit found home, kit ok, 8 worn kept,
  load 63%, Spirit Drinker IV up; bound, buffed 25/25, fighting again at 21:18:59.
- **Spirit Drinker (D143).** 23 casts, up all run. Flame Bolt VI on the Fire Sceptre, non-critical lines per target,
  round 13's Sceptre window (21:13-03:07 UTC, no aura) against this run's `spirit_drinker=up` hits: Tusker Guard 144.8
  (n=611) -> 150.8 (n=1,058), **+4.1% +/- 0.9%**, against ACE's +3.9% (caster modifier 1.03 -> 1.07). Tusker Slave
  301.2 -> 303.6, +0.8%, within noise (a killing bolt logs no damage line). Trip check 4's +12% is withdrawn: 49 hits,
  mixed casters and targets.
- **Damage taken,** all incoming lines: the robe, 14:38-21:10:26, 12,136 hits, 37,425 points (about 1,856 hits an
  hour); the owner, 21:10:26-21:17:35, 11 hits, 28 points; the robe again, 21:17:35-00:46, 6,071 hits, 18,850 points
  (about 1,745 an hour); mean 3.1 a hit both times. No armour-in-the-den window: the robe went back on before /auto.
- **The grant, said plainly (D146).** 10 Diamond Scarabs were granted at 14:37 UTC (1 held -> 11) through
  `--gear-grant-only=7299:10`, by the owner's exception to DoD 6 and D143 for this test run only. The ring burned 1, so
  10 were held at the end. By play alone the ring would have stopped the run at its one burn (1 held, under the stop
  line of 2): no shop sells 7299 by play (`m5-8-unsold-component-floor`).
- **The lead's check bug.** The 20-minute checks and the watches matched `\] ERROR`, which never matches a log line
  (`Z ERROR [cat]`): their "no errors" meant nothing until the fix at 20:10 UTC (godot_errors is a different count).
  Recounted with `Z ERROR `: round 13 4 (run 2's stop), the five checks of 2026-10-30 0, the 14:13 run 1 (its stop
  line), the long run 2.

### Item 7's end: every open issue, the item-end gates and the login pause (2026-10-31; D148 to D151)

**Every open issue before the merge (D148, D149).** The rows placed in item 8 on 2026-10-30 were done in item 7, each
on its own branch from the m5/7 tip, merged by the lead (section 9 says which commit closed each):

- **P7q, the spell icons (D147).** 86baabf2, merged 527e2caf. `UiSpellIcons` composes each spell as retail's
  `CompositeSpellIcon` (the power tile, the icon, the outline, the badge) and each component's white to black.
- **P7r, the toolbar and the packs.** 5ee76bf6, merged ea3ed150. The panel buttons are one radio group; the pack list
  is sized to its container, scrolls, and opens at its first row; `CheckOpenMarks` follows the open container.
- **P7s, the no-go cells and the unsold component.** 86da5085, merged f9823c3f. No corpse Use whose ACE approach enters
  a no-go cell; a step for an attacker inside one; a component no shop sells stops the run at its floor (D148 amended:
  Pyreal, Platinum and Gold Scarabs stop at their floor; Silver is bought from Cindrue, 7fd26839).
- **P7t, the heavy finds (D149).** 44a9b2b8, merged 9be6eff6. A find over 150 burden is kept aside only as a wearable
  upgrade (`SelfEquipJudge`).

**The item-end gates** (`cache/gate/item-end*` in the item worktree; fresh characters, D44/D45):

- **item-end (01:52 UTC) and item-end-2 (03:27 UTC)** found the gate's own failures, each fixed on its branch:
  P7o (065140b6 and 73f51259: the vitals guard stands down for the scripted death; the lie-down is checked; the storeys
  win reads `storeys_visited`), P7p (d74acc3c: Renald's stand at (133.9, 15.7, 94), inside his 5 m use radius, the old
  point 5.04 m out; the reopen after a stuck MoveTo; the heal judged on what it restored), P7u (a4667b46: the fighters'
  hunt fights back an attacker before it ends, D88) and P7v (17842320: one ground tour per journey, so the death wait
  walks to standing tuskers).
- **item-end-3-r2 (03:51-04:45 UTC, d3a410f6):** journey-mage 12/12, autopilot-mage, journey-melee 9/9,
  journey-archer 10/10 and the Holtburg walk (2 of 3 clean) passed. The plain login check failed (131.3, 51.8 and
  74.4 ms), so no merge (the owner's rule), and the backend was stopped for the reboot (D150).

**The login pause (D151).** The shell build allocated the same 18-22 MiB as in every earlier gate. The collection that
closes its no-GC window took 75 and 135 ms (10-22 ms before), and the run's GC pauses in all were 3-5 times the earlier
gates' with the same counts. A baseline after the reboot (`gc-base-r2`) and 9 single logins (`stall-diag`) were mostly
normal, but one collection still took 107.8 ms with about 94 ms of process CPU: it was collecting on too few threads.
The cause is server GC's dynamic heap count (DATAS). Eight logins under DATAS (`gc-exp/datas-*`): closing collections
10.9-107.8 ms, 3 of 8 over 50 ms. Eight with `DOTNET_GCHeapCount=8` (`gc-exp/heaps8-*`): 7.0-22.3 ms, every login at
most 39.1 ms, the GC pause in all 11-31 ms against 55-221 ms, managed memory 251-340 MB against 125-174. The launcher
sets it (2482c0ac, docs/game-core.md §7). The slow-frame line carries `render_cpu_ms`, `render_gpu_ms`, `physics_ms`
and `process_cpu_ms` (`RenderFrameWatch`). They show the rarer 74-83 ms frame (01:55, 03:28 and 12:25 UTC, and
`gc-exp/datas-8`) as a wait: no GC, no drawing, and little CPU. It is item 8's (`m5-8-login-walk-and-frames`).

**The login checks after the fix** (each three runs of the plain login and three of the Holtburg walk, alone):

- **d151-r2 (12:47-12:52 UTC, 2482c0ac):** the login 3 of 3 (33.3, 28.6, 27.4 ms). The walk 2 of 3: run 1 was refused
  "not in the world yet" by the server mod 5 s after LoginComplete (`m5-8-place-not-in-world`, D152).
- **d152-r2 (13:04-13:09 UTC):** the login 3 of 3 (25.6, 32.8, 28.8 ms). The walk 2 of 3: run 1 stood still 2.95 m
  short of the Academy exit's first goal with no key held. The window had lost the focus and `RouteDriver` never
  checked its keys (D153).
- **d153-r2 (13:19-13:25 UTC, a9bae9a6, the walker's fix):** the login 3 of 3 (28.2, 28.1, 33.6 ms) and the walk 3 of 3
  (enter to walk 24.8, 31.3 and 24.8 ms; Holtburg at 236, 240 and 223 fps; teleport frames 13.8, 18.3 and 15.1 ms),
  0 error lines. In run 1 the window lost the focus twice, and the walker pressed its keys again both times.

**The item-end check** (`slice-check -Changed -AllSizes`, backend down). Its first run (13:26-13:52 UTC, a9bae9a6)
failed 3 steps on the stale kit check (D154). The rerun (13:57-14:25 UTC, 52a7a5ea) passed every step at every size:
build, 11,674 tests (Net 1,109, Data 1,059, Loopback 190, ServerMod 609, Client 8,707; 0 failed), the play-checks
220 of 220 at 800x600 and 1280x720, the shell frames at three sizes, record-audio, the retail UI, the portal space,
the golden viewpoints and the fly path (max 19.1 ms). The test floor goes from 10,636 to 11,674 (Exhaustive stays at
775: that stage did not run). The suites in between showed only the known flakes that pass alone (PathPlanner's
one-kilometre timing; ManaOrderTests' allocation once).


### Item 8, "The end" (2026-10-31; D155 to D158)

Branch `m5/8` from master 3eb85bd8. The weekly meter read 78% at the start (14:33 UTC) and 80% after the gate (17:26
UTC); the share was 3% in section 3, estimated at about 5% with the three open rows.

**Part B, the rows placed in item 8** (three Sonnet slices side by side, each from m5/8 at 3eb85bd8):

- **place, `m5-8-place-not-in-world` (D152).** a138b083, merged cb19629e. `ToolTracker` holds a player-scope tool
  request while the client is out of the world and, on the mod's `not-in-world`, probes `where` for up to 20 s and
  resends once (`PlayerToolsWorldWaitTests`). ACE's cause read in its source: a player does not keep its landblock
  alive, so a login about 5 minutes after the last logout can join a landblock that unloads in that tick and leave the
  player in none (Landblock.cs:532-549); the wait then fails named, and the recovery is M6's (`m5-8-orphaned-login`).
- **npc, the step-around (`m5-8-login-walk-and-frames`, `TODO(holtburg-ring-stall)`).** 14b5c1a7, merged 191ce9a2.
  The Pumpkin Buffer (wcid 32205, a sphere of 0.48 m) stood at goal 7 of the Holtburg ring; `RouteDriver` now detours
  round a solid cylinder or sphere on the free side a placement probe finds, at most twice a goal
  (`RouteDriverObstacleTests`, `PumpkinBufferShapeTests`). Live: every lap's goal 7 detoured round it (five detour lines
  in a run) and the ring finished in all three runs. The holtburg-ring-stall's two causes are closed (the lost keys by
  D153); the rare offline timing stall stays `m5-offline-walkcheck-rate`.
- **build, the player build and the slow frame.** efafe95a, merged 22f4e983. The player build runs as four steps on
  four frames (`StagedWork`, `PlayerRig.BeginBuild`; `StagedWorkTests`); live `build_steps=4/4`, the largest step 6.5 ms
  (16.2 ms in all). The rare 74-83 ms frame read over 82 login logs is a wait outside the work (process time 4.5-10 ms,
  render CPU 0.15 ms, GPU 0.03 ms, no GC, one clock tick of process CPU): 9 of 25 logs before D151, 1 of 26 after; the
  code is unchanged and the next measure is `m5-8-idle-slow-frame` (M6).

**Part A, the milestone's end.** Three cluster reviews (Opus, read-only; DoD 1-5, 6-10, 11-15, against M3's and M4's
six or seven, for the meter) and a lookup of m4-plan.md section 9's rows placed M5 (Haiku), then one doc pass

(`m5-buff-effects-drawn`, a6bd30d3) merged into m5/8 (5ce758c3).

trips=1 bought=117 storeys_visited=5 basement_entries=0 interrupted=1 resumed=1 reconnects=0` (1,118 s), journey-melee

**The hero video (D24, D158).** One fresh take after the merge (2026-11-01 01:13-01:34 UTC, master with the buff-effects fix): BSD Mage on the autopilot for 20 minutes from Holtburg (`--autopilot-start --autopilot-minutes=20`, `done ok=yes kills=65 deaths=0`, 0 `Z ERROR ` lines), the 1920x1080 window captured by ffmpeg gfxcapture at 60 fps (M4's window method) and the game's own sound by `--record-audio`, the speakers muted. The take's white sync frame and its sound gave the offset: the sound leads by 0.800 s (the logged clocks said 0.788 s). The cut is 26.0 s of eight hard cuts with no text on screen: the buff pass at Holtburg's lifestone (the self-buff orbs the side session fixed), the recall, the portal tunnel, the arrival in the den, the run, Inferno's Gift and a Flame Volley, a fire burst among Tusker Guards, and Cassius' Ring of Fire with the mage surrounded. Picture and sound measured at 26.000 s each; the sync measured through the same pipeline on the marker: 0 ms. Every shot checked for held items and models (the wand in hand, no stray models); a shot that opened inside a tusker's body was trimmed. 1080p60 (32 MB) and 720p (9 MB) under `cache/video/m5-hero/` (gitignored: derived from the dats). The long run's own capture (silent, before the fixes) was not needed. The tooltip side session had not merged; its fix shows only on a hover, which the autopilot never does.

**The buff effects side item's closure (2026-10-31).** Closed: `m5-buff-effects-drawn` (the owner saw a Pumpkin Buffer's
Incantation of Armor Other draw the grey shell around the character on the Holtburg walk, while the mage's own buffs drew
nothing). A live pass with every line due counted the F755s on the player: none for the 20 creature and life casts, one
for each armour line. The client sent a SelfTargeted spell untargeted (0x0048), and ACE plays a cast's target effect only
for a cast that names a target. Retail's `ClientMagicSystem::CastSpell` (11.6096 0x00568DE0) sends such a spell at the
player's own id, which `CastTracker.SendTarget` now does: the same pass counts 28 effects for 26 casts, and the review
frames show the blue skill orbs and the grey shell on the mage (cache/buff-effects/, gitignored). Pinned by
`CastTrackerTests.A_self_buff_sends_0x004A_at_the_player_...` and `The_send_target_follows_retail_s_CastSpell`; the
creature orbs' early end at the autopilot's pace stays retail's (D100, `BuffEmitterIdTests`).
